# Proposed OEIS comments: A204750 to A207144

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A204750

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A204750>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2932](../papers/2501-3000/2932-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-3) for n > 5.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(188 - 348*x - 29*x^2 + 166*x^3 + 4*x^4) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Jun 09 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 4 096 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A204754

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A204754>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2543](../papers/2501-3000/2543-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for columns:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 2 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A205248

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A205248>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3175](../papers/3001-3500/3175-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A206839

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A206839>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2862](../papers/2501-3000/2862-PROOF.pdf)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 21 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207020

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207020>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2785](../papers/2501-3000/2785-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*n^3 + (27/2)*n^2 - (21/2)*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 17 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 91 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---


## A207106

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207106>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2831](../papers/2501-3000/2831-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = (4/3)*n^3 + 8*n^2 - (10/3)*n.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 20 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 43 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A207142

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A207142>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [8464](../papers/8001-8500/8464-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = a(n-1) + 2*a(n-2) - 2*a(n-3) for n>8.
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jun 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 8. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
